## Block if you find any of these

### WCAG drift (codex M4 r1 precedent)
- ANY ratio in colors.md that doesn't match `wcag_m5.py` output to two decimal places → BLOCKING.
- ANY pair where the AA verdict (PASS/FAIL) disagrees with the ratio computation → BLOCKING.
- ANY non-text pair below SC 1.4.11 3:1 threshold (without documented decorative justification) → BLOCKING.

### Token discipline (codex M2b r1 #3 precedent)
- Any new `--*` token in design.md that doesn't exist in tokens.css → BLOCKING.
- Hex isolation drift (must equal 24) → BLOCKING.
- Token count drift (must equal 83) → BLOCKING.

### Motion budget (codex M2b r1 #2 precedent)
- ANY transition on color, border-color, width, height, top, padding, transform-on-message-panels, background-color-on-message-panels → BLOCKING.
- Stagger keyframe must animate ONLY transform + opacity (NO top/padding/width/height).
- `translateX` vs `translateY` (spec line 93 says `translateX`): designer intentionally diverged. Decide if this is acceptable design rationale or blocking spec literal.

### Spec literal (codex M3a r1/r4 precedent)
- "Agent reaction (N messages)" — verify the design template renders ALL N (no special-cased `(1 message)` singular).
- "Agent-only session ({count} messages)" — same pluralization rule.
- "Oversized user message ({sizeKB} KB) — collapsed by default" verbatim.
- "Summary disabled — generation deferred to a later phase. Use \"Expand to raw messages\" to read the agent's response inline." verbatim.
- "SESSION RESUMED" / "CONVERSATION COMPACTED" verbatim uppercase.

### Boundary chapter-break recipe sharing
- Spec line 691 mandates byte-equivalence between Skim's boundary and Transcript's boundary at M5 close (signature detail #1).
- Verify design.md proposes a shared `BoundaryRow` component (not duplicated implementation).
- Verify the shape: flat `<li role="separator" aria-orientation="horizontal">` (matches M4 TranscriptView.tsx:370-371).

### a11y
- SkimView root: `<section aria-label="...">` landmark.
- Block list: ordered list semantics.
- `<details>` summary as accessible label.
- Scoped TranscriptView a11y inherited.

### Bun-first invariant (project-wide)
- No `child_process`, no `npm`/`node` references in the design.

### Protected paths
- No design artifact references modifications to `apps/backend/`, `components/`, `tests/e2e/` (Rust), root `Cargo.{toml,lock}`.

# Distill Portal Docs

Start here when you need to orient yourself in the Distill Portal workspace. The backend is Rust under `apps/backend`; the frontend is Bun + Vite + React + TypeScript under `apps/frontend`.

This file is both the docs entry point and the repo map.

## Start With Your Task

| I want to... | Start here |
| --- | --- |
| Modify the inspection UI | [`playbooks/modify-frontend-page.md`](playbooks/modify-frontend-page.md), [`../apps/frontend/README.md`](../apps/frontend/README.md) |
| Change a backend API or JSON payload | [`playbooks/modify-backend-api.md`](playbooks/modify-backend-api.md), [`dependency-rules.md`](dependency-rules.md) |
| Change session storage behavior | [`playbooks/modify-session-store.md`](playbooks/modify-session-store.md) |
| Run or test locally | [`dev-commands.md`](dev-commands.md) |
| Check dependency boundaries | [`dependency-rules.md`](dependency-rules.md) |
| Understand a feature | [`features/inspection-surface.md`](features/inspection-surface.md), [`features/session-store.md`](features/session-store.md) |
| Understand product + architecture intent | [`../PRD.md`](../PRD.md), [`../ARCHITECTURE.md`](../ARCHITECTURE.md) |
| Audit historical planning or delivery | [`../working/README.md`](../working/README.md), [`../progress/README.md`](../progress/README.md) |

## Apps

- `apps/backend` — Rust backend binary: runtime wiring, scan/ingest/store orchestration, machine-consumable HTTP routes. See [`../apps/backend/README.md`](../apps/backend/README.md).
- `apps/frontend` — Bun + Vite + React + TypeScript inspection UI. The unified session list (table, filters, view, drawer body, merge / filter / sort / paginate / persistence helpers) lives under `apps/frontend/src/features/sessions/`; shared React primitives (action bar, scan-errors callout, native-`<dialog>`-backed drawer shell, pagination strip, toast) live under `apps/frontend/src/components/`. CSS is co-located: each `.tsx`/`.ts` imports its sibling `.css` (no CSS Modules, no CSS-in-JS, no Tailwind); `src/styles/` carries only the three global sheets `reset.css`, `tokens.css`, `global.css`. See [`../apps/frontend/README.md`](../apps/frontend/README.md).

## Components

Reusable, app-independent crates. Each owns its own README:

- [`collector-runtime`](../components/collector-runtime/README.md) — source discovery, safe JSONL reads, tool-specific parsers (Claude Code, Codex)
- [`configuration`](../components/configuration/README.md) — backend runtime config loading
- [`ingest-service`](../components/ingest-service/README.md) — content-addressed ingest decisions, replace-on-sync
- [`observability`](../components/observability/README.md) — tracing subscriber bootstrap
- [`raw-session-store`](../components/raw-session-store/README.md) — SQLite metadata + blob-store persistence
- [`ui-api-contracts`](../components/ui-api-contracts/README.md) — shared HTTP payload types; source of truth for the Rust → TS bindings

## Feature Guides

- [`features/inspection-surface.md`](features/inspection-surface.md) — page ownership, API touchpoints, and tests for the inspection workflow
- [`features/session-store.md`](features/session-store.md) — storage ownership, backend touchpoints, and tests for persisted session behavior

## Playbooks

Short recipes for common changes:

- [`playbooks/modify-frontend-page.md`](playbooks/modify-frontend-page.md) — shortest safe path for UI changes
- [`playbooks/modify-backend-api.md`](playbooks/modify-backend-api.md) — shortest safe path for backend API changes
- [`playbooks/modify-session-store.md`](playbooks/modify-session-store.md) — shortest safe path for storage changes

# Distill Portal

Distill Portal is a local-first portal for collecting, storing, and analyzing coding sessions produced by AI coding tools.

Read [PRD.md](PRD.md) for the product summary and functional requirements.

For developers, please read [docs/README.md](docs/README.md) for documentation on the repository structure, component ownership, features, and development commands.

## Contributing

- Read [PRD.md](PRD.md) for the product summary and functional requirements.
- Read [ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md) for the architecture.
- Read [docs/README.md](docs/README.md) for the repository structure, component ownership, features, and development commands.

### Hard Rules

- Backend owns machine-consumable HTTP routes; do not reintroduce backend-owned end-user HTML.
- Frontend talks to the backend only over HTTP and only consumes generated contract types from `components/ui-api-contracts/bindings/*.ts`.
- `apps/frontend` must not import from `components/collector-runtime`, `components/ingest-service`, or `components/raw-session-store`.
- HTTP payload changes must update `components/ui-api-contracts`, the Rust and TS sides of both apps, and relevant tests together.
- Keep docs aligned with any layout, command, dependency, or payload-shape change.

### Quick Checks

Fast checks to run before committing:

```bash
cargo check --workspace
cargo test --workspace
```

From `apps/frontend/`:

```bash
bun run test
```

See [docs/dev-commands.md](docs/dev-commands.md) for the full reference, including targeted Rust suites, the TypeScript contract-drift check, and the browser e2e suite.

## Quick Start

Run the backend:

```bash
cargo run -p distill-portal-backend
```

Run the frontend (from `apps/frontend/`):

```bash
bun install
bun run dev
```

Then open the frontend address. By default:

- backend: `http://127.0.0.1:4000`
- frontend (Vite dev server): `http://127.0.0.1:4100`

See `docs/dev-commands.md` for the full list of frontend and test commands.

# Dev Commands

## Run The Backend

```bash
cargo run -p distill-portal-backend
```

Useful backend env vars:

- `DISTILL_PORTAL_BACKEND_BIND` or `DISTILL_PORTAL_BIND`
- `DISTILL_PORTAL_DATA_DIR`
- `DISTILL_PORTAL_POLL_INTERVAL_SECS`
- `DISTILL_PORTAL_CLAUDE_ROOTS`
- `DISTILL_PORTAL_CODEX_ROOTS`

## Run The Frontend

The frontend lives under `apps/frontend/` as a Bun-managed package (`package.json`, `bun.lock`, `vite.config.ts`, `index.html`, `src/*.tsx`).

Commands (run from `apps/frontend/`):

```bash
bun install
bun run dev
bun run build
bun run test
```

- `bun run dev` starts the Vite dev server on `http://127.0.0.1:4100` with `strictPort: true` so a port collision fails fast.
- `bun run build` writes static assets to `apps/frontend/dist/`.
- `bun run test` runs `bun test src` (the unit suite; browser e2e is `bun run test:e2e`, documented below). After Phase 4 the unit suite is 231 tests / 891 expect calls across 17 files covering the unified session list, the session detail drawer, pagination, the toast queue, and the shared React primitives:
  - **Mounted-`App` integration** — `apps/frontend/src/App.test.tsx`: the read-only unified-list render against three `Promise.allSettled` GETs, the rescan and import mutation flows (including an explicit race-window reproducer for the stale-selection bug), per-panel independent-error behavior, the four documented empty-state branches, the click-time intersection rule under filter and pagination races, the toast queue replacing the old inline `lastReport`, and the cross-page bulk-select / retry-after-intervening-rescan invariants.
  - **Unified session feature** — `apps/frontend/src/features/sessions/`: `SessionsTable.test.tsx`, `SessionFilters.test.tsx`, `SessionDetail.test.tsx` (component-level integration), and pure helpers `mergeSessions.test.ts`, `filterSessions.test.ts`, `applyPagination.test.ts`, `relativeTime.test.ts`, `useSessionFilters.test.ts` (incl. 12+ corrupt-input fallbacks), `useToastQueue.test.ts`, `lastRescan.test.ts`, and the streaming `rawPreview.test.ts` (line-cap + byte-cap + abort + non-JSON fallback).
  - **Shared React primitives** — `apps/frontend/src/components/`: `ActionBar.test.tsx` (idle zero/non-zero selection, pending rescan/import disabled states, sticky modifier, last-rescan caption, hidden-by-filter caption + clear affordances), `Drawer.test.tsx` (native `<dialog>` shell, focus restoration, Esc-close, backdrop close), `Pagination.test.tsx` (page-size selector + Prev/Next + caption + clamp), `Toast.test.tsx` (kind variants + Retry + Dismiss roles), `ScanErrorsCallout.test.tsx` (renders nothing when empty).
  - The variant-matrix `StatusBadge.test.tsx` from M2 was retired in M6 (Chunk G); the four-variant DOM-shape contract is preserved by a dedicated test in `SessionsTable.test.tsx` (`SessionsTable: inlined status badge renders all 4 variants ...`) that iterates over `up_to_date / not_stored / outdated / source_missing` and asserts the inlined `<span class="badge {variant}">{label}</span>` markup byte-for-byte for each variant. `App.test.tsx` continues to assert the `up-to-date` variant at the integration level.

## Dev Topology

```
browser
   |
   v
http://127.0.0.1:4100  <-- Vite dev server (bun run dev)
   |
   |  /api/v1/**  and  /health   proxied by Vite
   v
http://127.0.0.1:4000  <-- Rust backend (cargo run -p distill-portal-backend)
```

- Backend: `127.0.0.1:4000` (Rust, `cargo run -p distill-portal-backend`).
- Frontend dev server: `127.0.0.1:4100` (Bun + Vite, `bun run dev` under `apps/frontend/`).
- Vite proxies `/api/v1/**` and `/health` to the backend so browser code can use same-origin paths without ad hoc CORS setup. Dev-time proxy config lives in `apps/frontend/vite.config.ts`, not in application code.

## Run Both Together

Terminal 1:

```bash
cargo run -p distill-portal-backend
```

Terminal 2 (from `apps/frontend/`):

```bash
bun run dev
```

Then open `http://127.0.0.1:4100/` in the browser. The Rust backend must be running for `/health` and `/api/v1/**` to resolve through the Vite proxy.

## Browser E2E (Playwright)

The inspection-surface browser e2e lives under `apps/frontend/e2e/` and
drives Chromium against the Bun + Vite dev server, which proxies to a
real Rust backend spawned inside the harness (`e2e/harness/backend.ts`).
The harness uses `Bun.spawn` per the Bun-first rule, so the suite must
be run with Bun as the runtime (`bun --bun x playwright test`, wired
into the `test:e2e` script below).

Commands (run from `apps/frontend/`):

```bash
bun run test:e2e:install   # one-time: install Chromium via Playwright
bun run test:e2e           # spawns the backend, starts Vite, runs Chromium
```

The harness binds the backend to `127.0.0.1:4000` because
`vite.config.ts` proxies `/api/v1/**` and `/health` to that address;
tests run serially (`workers: 1`) so the port is never double-bound.
See `playwright.config.ts` for the full configuration.

## Targeted Tests

```bash
cargo test -p distill-portal-collector-runtime --test parsers
cargo test -p distill-portal-backend --test http_api
cargo test -p distill-portal-e2e --test inspection_surface
```

## TypeScript Contract Bindings

The Rust contract types in `components/ui-api-contracts/src/lib.rs` are the source of truth. TypeScript declarations under `components/ui-api-contracts/bindings/` are generated from them via the `ts-bindings` cargo feature (off by default) and must be regenerated whenever the Rust contract changes.

Verify the checked-in TS files match the current Rust source (fails on drift):

```bash
cargo test -p distill-portal-ui-api-contracts --features ts-bindings
```

Regenerate the checked-in TS files after a contract change:

```bash
cargo test -p distill-portal-ui-api-contracts --features ts-bindings -- --ignored regenerate_ts_bindings
```

See `components/ui-api-contracts/README.md` for the tool-choice rationale and the full list of generated files.

## Workspace Verification

```bash
cargo check --workspace
cargo test --workspace
```

# Dependency Rules

## Allowed Directions

- `apps/backend` may depend on:
  - `components/collector-runtime`
  - `components/configuration`
  - `components/ingest-service`
  - `components/observability`
  - `components/raw-session-store`
  - `components/ui-api-contracts`
- `apps/frontend` (Bun + Vite + React + TypeScript) may depend on:
  - `components/ui-api-contracts/bindings/*.ts` (generated TypeScript types; one-way import only)
  - external npm packages managed via Bun for UI, HTTP fetch, and test tooling. Phase 4 adopted a strict 2-package escape-hatch budget for browser-only concerns native APIs cannot meet; one slot is consumed (`focus-trap-react@^11`, fixing a native `<dialog>` Tab-cycling gap), one slot remains reserved (`@tanstack/react-virtual`, gated on documented evidence that pagination plus `useMemo` cannot keep render time bounded). Adding a third escape-hatch package requires a fresh planning round.
  - all backend communication is over HTTP to `apps/backend`
- `components/ingest-service` may depend on:
  - `components/collector-runtime`
  - `components/raw-session-store`
- `components/raw-session-store` may depend on:
  - `components/ui-api-contracts`
- `components/collector-runtime` may depend on:
  - `components/ui-api-contracts`
- `components/configuration`, `components/observability`, and `components/ui-api-contracts` should not depend on app crates

## Forbidden Directions

- `apps/frontend` must not depend on:
  - `components/collector-runtime`
  - `components/ingest-service`
  - `components/raw-session-store`
- No component crate may depend on `apps/backend` or `apps/frontend`
- `apps/backend` must not render end-user HTML
- `apps/frontend` must not reach into storage or ingest internals directly

## Contract Handling

- Shared page and JSON payloads live in `components/ui-api-contracts`
- `components/ui-api-contracts/src/lib.rs` is the single source of truth for the contract. The TypeScript declarations under `components/ui-api-contracts/bindings/` are checked-in generated artifacts derived from `src/lib.rs` by the `ts-bindings` cargo feature — they are the canonical downstream output and must stay in sync with the Rust source: regenerate and commit them together with any Rust contract change, never hand-edited
- The Bun frontend consumes contract types via one-way TypeScript imports from `components/ui-api-contracts/bindings/*.ts`, using the `@contracts/*` path alias wired in `apps/frontend/tsconfig.json` (or a direct relative path). The frontend MUST NOT re-declare contract types by hand; the thin barrel at `apps/frontend/src/lib/contracts.ts` re-exports the generated types for internal consumption
- If a backend JSON shape changes, update:
  - `components/ui-api-contracts/src/lib.rs`
  - `components/ui-api-contracts/bindings/*.ts` (regenerate via `cargo test -p distill-portal-ui-api-contracts --features ts-bindings -- --ignored regenerate_ts_bindings`; see `docs/dev-commands.md`)
  - `apps/backend/src/http_api.rs`
  - the typed API layer in `apps/frontend/src/lib/` (`api.ts`, `contracts.ts`)
  - any impacted frontend rendering under `apps/frontend/src/App.tsx`, the unified session feature at `apps/frontend/src/features/sessions/`, or the shared React primitives under `apps/frontend/src/components/`
  - the relevant tests in `apps/backend/tests/http_api.rs`, `apps/frontend/src/App.test.tsx`, `apps/frontend/e2e/inspection.spec.ts`, and `tests/e2e/tests/inspection_surface.rs`

## Layer Ownership

- Storage writes and reads belong to `components/raw-session-store`
- Replace-on-sync ingest rules belong to `components/ingest-service`
- Source discovery and parsing belong to `components/collector-runtime`
- Environment variable mapping belongs to `components/configuration`
- HTTP routes belong to the app crates only

## Frontend Dev-Time Topology

- `apps/frontend/` is a Bun-managed package (`package.json`, `bun.lock`, `vite.config.ts`, `index.html`, `src/*.tsx`).
- Frontend dev-time proxying of `/api/v1/**` and `/health` to the backend belongs in `apps/frontend/vite.config.ts`, not in any application code path.
- The Bun app must continue to honor the frontend-boundary rules above: no direct dependency on `components/collector-runtime`, `components/ingest-service`, or `components/raw-session-store`; all backend communication goes over HTTP to the Rust backend.

### Phase 5 M2a notes — Fraunces self-hosted as static asset

Phase 5 introduces a single self-hosted variable serif (Fraunces) for the Archive-room display layer (empty-pane preface, future M5 chapter-break labels, future app title). The font ships as two `.woff2` files at `apps/frontend/public/fonts/Fraunces-subset.woff2` (Roman) and `apps/frontend/public/fonts/Fraunces-Italic-subset.woff2` (Italic), referenced by two `@font-face` blocks in `apps/frontend/src/styles/tokens.css`. Per Phase 5 §Resolved Decision #15, **a self-hosted font file is a static asset, not a runtime dependency** — the 2-package escape-hatch budget (currently 1/2 consumed by `focus-trap-react@^11`) is **not affected**. No bundler plugin, font-loading library, or JS dep is involved; Vite serves the woff2 files directly from `public/`. To regenerate (only when upstream Fraunces revs, ~yearly per Google Fonts cadence): download both variable fonts from `https://github.com/undercasetype/Fraunces` (`fonts/variable/Fraunces[SOFT,WONK,opsz,wght].ttf` + the Italic counterpart), run `fonttools varLib.instancer <input.ttf> SOFT=50 WONK=0 -o <pinned.ttf>` to drop the design-time-only SOFT and WONK axes (saves ~70 KB per file), then `pyftsubset <pinned.ttf> --output-file=apps/frontend/public/fonts/Fraunces-subset.woff2 --unicodes='U+0020-007E,U+00A0-00FF,U+2010-2027,U+2030-2052' --layout-features='kern,liga,smcp,onum' --flavor=woff2 --desubroutinize`. Target file size: ~80 KB per file. Reversibility: deleting both `@font-face` blocks AND both woff2 files yields a still-cohesive system-serif aesthetic (Charter / Iowan Old Style / Georgia at ~99% of Fraunces's visual size via the `size-adjust` override, no broken layout).

# M5 WCAG AA contrast verification

> **All ratios computed via `wcag_m5.py`** (committed in this folder).
> The script is the deterministic source of truth. Every ratio in the
> tables below is the verbatim script output, transcribed to two
> decimal places (the script's own format string `:>7.2f`). Re-run
> with:
>
>     python3 working/phase-5/designs/m5-skim/wcag_m5.py
>
> (or `bun --bun python wcag_m5.py`).
>
> Pure Python stdlib — no third-party dependencies. Computes oklch ->
> oklab -> linear sRGB -> nonlinear sRGB -> relative luminance per CSS
> Color L4 §10 + WCAG 2.1 §1.4.3, with `color-mix(in srgb, ...)`
> performed in nonlinear sRGB space (Chromium parity).
>
> **Rounding rule**: 2 decimal places, truncated as the Python format
> spec `:>7.2f` rounds (banker's rounding — IEEE 754
> round-half-to-even).

This is the contrast table for every visible foreground/background
pair the M5 chunk introduces. M5 adds NO new color tokens AND NO new
color recipes — every pair below reuses an M2a-canonical token in a
recipe ALREADY measured in M4's colors.md. The M5 pairs are
re-measured here by the M5 script (`wcag_m5.py` shares math + token
table with `wcag_m4.py` byte-equivalent), confirming byte-equivalent
ratios on shared pairs.

The M4-equivalence column documents which M4 pair ID each M5 pair
maps to. Codex re-running `python3 wcag_m5.py` should observe the M5
ratios are byte-identical to the M4 ratios for shared pairs (drift =
math regression, not design change).

## Methodology (committed script)

The M5 deliverable commits the WCAG verification script at
`working/phase-5/designs/m5-skim/wcag_m5.py`. The script is
byte-equivalent in math + matrices + TOKENS to `wcag_m4.py`; only
the PAIRS list differs. Rationale: M5 introduces ZERO new tokens, so
the token table must match M4 exactly. The script:

1. Hardcodes the M2a-canonical oklch values for every token (in the
   `TOKENS` dict at the top of `wcag_m5.py`). Identical to
   `wcag_m4.py`.
2. Converts each value via the canonical CSS Color L4 pipeline:
   `oklch -> oklab -> linear sRGB -> nonlinear sRGB -> relative
   luminance`. The oklab -> linear-sRGB step uses Bjorn Ottosson's
   M2 matrix.
3. Computes the WCAG 2.1 contrast ratio:
   `((L1 + 0.05) / (L2 + 0.05))` where L1 ≥ L2 are relative
   luminances per WCAG 2.1 §1.4.3.
4. For `color-mix(in srgb, ...)` recipes, performs the mix in
   nonlinear sRGB space (matching CSS `color-mix(in srgb)`
   semantics — Chromium parity) before computing relative luminance.

The script is **pure Python stdlib** (only `math`); no third-party
deps. Re-run with `python3 wcag_m5.py` or `bun --bun python wcag_m5.py`.
A future codex round verifying this artifact should re-run the script
on its own machine and compare the table values.

The script computes 29 pairs covering every M5-visible
foreground/background combination (S01..S29). All ratios are
byte-equivalent to M4's measurements where the recipe matches; the
M4-equivalence column documents the M4 pair ID for cross-check.

## WCAG AA gates

- **4.5:1** for normal text (< 18 pt or < 14 pt bold).
- **3.0:1** for large text (≥ 18 pt or ≥ 14 pt bold) AND for non-text
  UI components per SC 1.4.11 ("non-text" = NT in the Bar column).

Every text pair below is treated as normal text at its declared
font-size token (no surface uses ≥ 18 pt or ≥ 14 pt bold). The
boundary rule, the warn stripes, the focus-visible outlines, and
decorative borders are non-text per SC 1.4.11.

## M5 surface map (light + dark)

The columns: # / M4-equiv / Surface (M5 component) / Foreground /
Background / Light contrast / Dark contrast / Verdict / Bar.

Ratios are byte-equivalent to M4's `colors.md` (codex round-2
script-authoritative). Where an M5 pair maps to an M4 pair by recipe,
the row is annotated with the M4 ID; the script-output ratios are
identical (the script reuses the same TOKENS + math).

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""WCAG 2.1 contrast ratio computer for M5 SkimView surfaces.

Usage:
    python3 wcag_m5.py
    bun --bun python wcag_m5.py

Pure-stdlib (only `math`); no third-party deps. Reads no files; emits a
table for every pair declared in colors.md.

Pipeline (matches CSS Color L4 sec 10 + WCAG 2.1 sec 1.4.3):
    1. oklch -> oklab
    2. oklab -> linear sRGB (via Bjorn Ottosson's M2 matrix)
    3. linear sRGB -> nonlinear sRGB (piecewise gamma per IEC 61966-2-1)
    4. color-mix(in srgb, A p%, B q%): mix in nonlinear sRGB space, then
       reverse step 3 to get back to linear sRGB.
    5. Relative luminance: linear-sRGB R*0.2126 + G*0.7152 + B*0.0722
    6. Contrast: (L1 + 0.05) / (L2 + 0.05) where L1 >= L2.

This matches Chromium's `color-mix(in srgb)` implementation. The script
is byte-equivalent in math + matrices to wcag_m4.py; only the PAIRS list
differs. Rationale: M5 introduces ZERO new tokens, so the token table
must match M4 exactly. Drift between M4 and M5 ratios on shared pairs
(e.g. ink-on-surface) would indicate a math regression, not a design
change. Codex round 1 should re-run this script and diff its output
against M4's for shared pairs.
"""

import math
from typing import Tuple

# ----- M2a-canonical token oklch values (byte-equivalent to wcag_m4.py) -----
TOKENS = {
    "light": {
        "surface":        (0.98, 0.01, 70),
        "surface-raised": (0.96, 0.01, 70),
        "ink":            (0.20, 0.02, 70),
        "ink-muted":      (0.45, 0.02, 70),
        "border":         (0.85, 0.01, 70),
        "border-strong":  (0.65, 0.02, 70),
        "accent":         (0.55, 0.15, 50),
        "warn":           (0.58, 0.15, 60),
        "error":          (0.52, 0.18, 25),
    },
    "dark": {
        "surface":        (0.15, 0.01, 70),
        "surface-raised": (0.18, 0.01, 70),
        "ink":            (0.92, 0.01, 70),
        "ink-muted":      (0.70, 0.01, 70),
        "border":         (0.28, 0.01, 70),
        "border-strong":  (0.48, 0.02, 70),
        "accent":         (0.65, 0.15, 50),
        "warn":           (0.72, 0.13, 60),
        "error":          (0.68, 0.16, 25),
    },
}


def oklch_to_oklab(L: float, C: float, h_deg: float) -> Tuple[float, float, float]:
    h = math.radians(h_deg)
    return (L, C * math.cos(h), C * math.sin(h))


def oklab_to_linear_srgb(L: float, a: float, b: float) -> Tuple[float, float, float]:
    # Bjorn Ottosson's matrices: oklab -> LMS' (cube-root applied) -> LMS -> linear sRGB
    l_ = L + 0.3963377774 * a + 0.2158037573 * b
    m_ = L - 0.1055613458 * a - 0.0638541728 * b
    s_ = L - 0.0894841775 * a - 1.2914855480 * b

    l = l_ ** 3
    m = m_ ** 3
    s = s_ ** 3

    r = +4.0767416621 * l - 3.3077115913 * m + 0.2309699292 * s
    g = -1.2684380046 * l + 2.6097574011 * m - 0.3413193965 * s
    b_ = -0.0041960863 * l - 0.7034186147 * m + 1.7076147010 * s
    return (r, g, b_)


def linear_to_srgb(c: float) -> float:
    # IEC 61966-2-1 piecewise; clamp first.
    c = max(0.0, min(1.0, c))
    if c <= 0.0031308:
        return 12.92 * c
    return 1.055 * (c ** (1 / 2.4)) - 0.055


def srgb_to_linear(c: float) -> float:
    c = max(0.0, min(1.0, c))
    if c <= 0.04045:
        return c / 12.92
    return ((c + 0.055) / 1.055) ** 2.4


def oklch_to_srgb(L: float, C: float, h: float) -> Tuple[float, float, float]:
    lab = oklch_to_oklab(L, C, h)
    rgb_lin = oklab_to_linear_srgb(*lab)
    return tuple(linear_to_srgb(x) for x in rgb_lin)


def relative_luminance_from_linear(r: float, g: float, b: float) -> float:
    return 0.2126 * r + 0.7152 * g + 0.0722 * b


def relative_luminance_oklch(L: float, C: float, h: float) -> float:
    srgb = oklch_to_srgb(L, C, h)
    rgb_lin = tuple(srgb_to_linear(c) for c in srgb)
    return relative_luminance_from_linear(*rgb_lin)


def color_mix_srgb(
    a_oklch: Tuple[float, float, float],
    a_pct: float,
    b_oklch: Tuple[float, float, float],
) -> float:
    """color-mix(in srgb, A p%, B q%) -> relative luminance of the mix.

    a_pct is fractional (e.g. 0.05 for 5%); b weight is (1 - a_pct).
    """
    a_srgb = oklch_to_srgb(*a_oklch)
    b_srgb = oklch_to_srgb(*b_oklch)
    mixed_srgb = tuple(a_pct * a_srgb[i] + (1 - a_pct) * b_srgb[i] for i in range(3))
    rgb_lin = tuple(srgb_to_linear(c) for c in mixed_srgb)
    return relative_luminance_from_linear(*rgb_lin)


def contrast(l1: float, l2: float) -> float:
    hi = max(l1, l2)
    lo = min(l1, l2)
    return (hi + 0.05) / (lo + 0.05)


# ----- Pair declarations -----
# Each pair: (id, label, foreground spec, background spec)
# Foreground/background spec is a string like "ink" or a tuple
# ("mix", "accent", 0.05, "surface").
#
# M5 introduces ZERO new tokens AND ZERO new color recipes. Every pair
# below either:
#   (a) reuses an M4 measurement byte-equivalent (same tokens, same recipe);
#   (b) measures an M5-specific text-on-surface combination (e.g. boundary
#       label on the fully-bare surface, or ink-muted on the user-tinted
#       background mix used by user_turn panels).
#
# Pairs S01..S29 are the M5 surfaces. Where a pair is byte-equivalent to
# an M4 pair, the inline comment names the M4 ID for cross-reference.

PAIRS = [
    # ---------- user_turn block ----------
    ("S01", "user_turn body text",                       "ink",       ("mix", "accent", 0.05, "surface")),  # = M4 T01
    ("S02", "user_turn code-fence body",                 "ink",       "surface-raised"),                    # = M4 T19/T20
    ("S03", "user_turn inline <code>",                   "ink",       "surface-raised"),                    # = M4 T19
    ("S04", "Agent reaction <summary> chrome",           "ink-muted", "surface"),                           # = M4 T05/T22
    ("S05", "Disabled placeholder prose",                "ink-muted", "surface"),                           # = M4 T22
    ("S06", "Disabled placeholder 4px left border (NT)", "border",    "surface"),                           # SC 1.4.11 NT (decorative aid)
    ("S07", "'Expand to raw messages' summary text",     "accent",    "surface"),                           # = M4 T07/T28
    ("S08", "'Expand to raw messages' hover underline",  "accent",    "surface"),                           # text-decoration only

]


def luminance_for(spec, mode: str) -> float:
    pal = TOKENS[mode]
    if isinstance(spec, str):
        return relative_luminance_oklch(*pal[spec])
    elif isinstance(spec, tuple) and spec[0] == "mix":
        _, a_name, a_pct, b_name = spec
        return color_mix_srgb(pal[a_name], a_pct, pal[b_name])
    raise ValueError(f"unknown spec: {spec}")


def main():
    print(f"{'#':<5} {'Surface':<48} {'FG':<14} {'BG':<32} {'Light':>9} {'Dark':>9}")
    print("-" * 122)
    for pair_id, label, fg, bg in PAIRS:
        ratios = []
        for mode in ("light", "dark"):
            l_fg = luminance_for(fg, mode)
            l_bg = luminance_for(bg, mode)
            ratios.append(contrast(l_fg, l_bg))
        bg_repr = bg if isinstance(bg, str) else f"mix({bg[1]} {int(bg[2]*100)}%, {bg[3]})"
        fg_repr = fg if isinstance(fg, str) else str(fg)
        print(f"{pair_id:<5} {label:<48} {fg_repr:<14} {bg_repr:<32} {ratios[0]:>7.2f}:1 {ratios[1]:>7.2f}:1")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

## Methodology footnote — `color-mix(in srgb, X p%, Y)` semantics

The `color-mix(in srgb, ...)` recipe per CSS Color L4 §4.1 mixes
two colors in the **nonlinear sRGB color space**. The committed
`wcag_m5.py` at `working/phase-5/designs/m5-skim/wcag_m5.py`
implements the pipeline byte-equivalent to `wcag_m4.py`:

1. Converts each oklch color to linear sRGB (via oklab — Bjorn
   Ottosson's M2 matrix).
2. Converts each linear sRGB triple to nonlinear sRGB via the
   piecewise function in CSS Color L4 §10.4.
3. Mixes by `p1 * sRGB1_channel + (1 - p1) * sRGB2_channel`.
4. Converts the mixed nonlinear sRGB back to linear sRGB.
5. Computes relative luminance via WCAG 2.1 §1.4.3.

This pipeline matches Chromium's implementation of `color-mix(in
srgb)`. Mixing directly in linear sRGB (skipping steps 2 + 4) gives
an answer that drifts by ~0.4-0.8 ratio points at the M5 alpha
values (5%, 8%) — the same magnitude codex caught in M4 round 2.
The script's nonlinear-sRGB-then-mix pipeline is authoritative.

script = {}
for line in out.splitlines():

    cells = [c.strip() for c in line.strip().strip('|').split('|')]

    if len(cells) >= 8 and re.fullmatch(r'\\d+\\.\\d{2}:1', cells[5]) and re.fullmatch(r'\\d+\\.\\d{2}:1', cells[6]):

missing_in_colors = sorted(set(script) - set(colors))
missing_in_script = sorted(set(colors) - set(script))
drifts = []
for sid in sorted(set(script) & set(colors)):
    sl, sd = script[sid]
    cl, cd = colors[sid]
    dl, dd = abs(sl - cl), abs(sd - cd)

print(f'missing_in_colors={missing_in_colors or \"none\"}')
print(f'missing_in_script={missing_in_script or \"none\"}')
print(f'drifts_over_0.005={drifts or \"none\"}')

   233	## Data Model in the Browser
   234	
   235	The unified inspection-list data model from Phase 4 is unchanged: `SessionRow` (18 fields), `isImportable`, `mergeSessions` join, filter/sort/pagination pipeline.
   236	
   237	Phase 5 adds a per-tool typed-message data model in `apps/frontend/src/features/sessions/parsers/types.ts`:
   238	
   239	```ts
   240	export type MessageKind =
   241	  | "user" | "assistant" | "tool_use"
   242	  | "tool_result" | "system" | "unknown"
   243	  | "boundary";   // Codex session_resumed / compacted markers; carried through to buildSkim
   244	
   245	export type Message = {
   246	  /** 0-indexed line number in the raw NDJSON. Multiple messages can share the same lineOrdinal when a single line splits into N message rows (e.g., Claude Code assistant content array with text + tool_use). */
   247	  lineOrdinal: number;
   248	  /** 0-indexed sequential position in messages[]. STABLE identity used by skim ranges and "Expand to raw messages" scoping. */
   249	  messageIndex: number;
   250	  timestamp: string | null;
   251	  kind: MessageKind;
   252	  text: string;
   253	  toolName?: string;      // populated when kind === "tool_use" or "tool_result"
   254	  /** Populated when kind === "boundary". buildSkim consumes this to label the chapter break. */
   255	  boundarySubtype?: "session_resumed" | "compacted";
   256	  raw: string;            // verbatim NDJSON line for "Expand to raw" affordances
   257	  bytes: number;          // approximate UTF-8 byte size for oversize detection
   258	};
   259	
   260	export type BlockKind =
   261	  | "user_turn" | "boundary" | "agent_only" | "oversized_user_message";
   262	
   263	export type SkimBlock = {
   264	  kind: BlockKind;
   265	  /** Inclusive messageIndex range (NOT lineOrdinal). The "Expand to raw messages" affordance slices messages[start..=end] to render a scoped TranscriptView. */
   266	  start: number;
   267	  end: number;
   268	  meta?: Record<string, string | number>;
   269	};
   270	
   271	export type ParseWarning = {
   272	  lineOrdinal: number;
   273	  reason: string;         // human-readable; surfaced as a small dismissible banner
   274	};
   275	
   276	/** What a per-tool parser returns. Pure of stream metadata. */
   277	export type ParserOutput = {
   278	  messages: Message[];
   279	  warnings: ParseWarning[];
   280	};
   281	
   282	/** What the dispatcher assembles by wrapping ParserOutput with stream metadata + buildSkim. Consumed by Skim/Transcript views. */
   283	export type ParsedSession = {
   284	  tool: Tool;
   285	  messages: Message[];
   286	  skim: SkimBlock[];
   287	  totalBytes: number;
   288	  truncated: boolean;     // true when the 5 MB cap fired during streamRawText
   289	  warnings: ParseWarning[];
   290	};
   291	
   292	/** Stream metadata produced by streamRawText. Passed into dispatchParser. */
   293	export type StreamMeta = {
   294	  totalBytes: number;
   295	  truncated: boolean;
   296	};
   297	```
   298	
   299	Per-tool parsers are pure, total, synchronous functions. They consume only `rawText` and emit `ParserOutput` (`{messages, warnings}`) — they have no knowledge of stream caps, byte counts, or truncation. The dispatcher wraps their output with stream metadata + `buildSkim` to produce the consumable `ParsedSession`.
   300	
   301	```ts
   302	export function parseClaudeCode(rawText: string): ParserOutput;
   303	export function parseCodex(rawText: string): ParserOutput;
   304	```
   305	
   306	Dispatch is a registry, not a switch — adding a third tool in a future phase is one record entry, not a control-flow edit:
   307	
   308	```ts
   309	// parsers/index.ts
   310	import type { Tool } from "../../../lib/contracts";
   311	import { parseClaudeCode } from "./claude_code";
   312	import { parseCodex } from "./codex";
   313	import { buildSkim, USER_MSG_OVERSIZE_THRESHOLD } from "./buildSkim";
   314	import type { ParserOutput, ParsedSession, StreamMeta } from "./types";
   315	
   316	export type ParserFn = (rawText: string) => ParserOutput;
   317	
   318	export const PARSERS: Record<Tool, ParserFn> = {
   319	  claude_code: parseClaudeCode,
   320	  codex: parseCodex,
   321	};
   322	
   323	export function dispatchParser(
   324	  tool: Tool,
   325	  rawText: string,
   326	  streamMeta: StreamMeta,
   327	): ParsedSession {
   328	  const parser = PARSERS[tool];
   329	  const output: ParserOutput = parser
   330	    ? parser(rawText)
   331	    : {
   332	        messages: [],
   333	        warnings: [
   334	          { lineOrdinal: 0, reason: `No parser registered for tool "${tool}"` },
   335	        ],
   336	      };
   337	  return {
   338	    tool,
   339	    messages: output.messages,
   340	    skim: buildSkim(output.messages, USER_MSG_OVERSIZE_THRESHOLD),
   341	    totalBytes: streamMeta.totalBytes,
   342	    truncated: streamMeta.truncated,
   343	    warnings: output.warnings,
   344	  };
   345	}
   346	```
   347	
   348	The registry typechecks via `Record<Tool, ParserFn>` — if the `Tool` union grows without a corresponding `PARSERS` entry, TypeScript fails the build. Tests assert exhaustiveness over `Object.keys(PARSERS)`.

     1	# M5 — SkimView (Phase 5 / Milestone 5)
     2	
     3	Design artifact for **Phase 5 / Milestone 5 / SkimView**.
     4	Spec frozen at `working/phase-5.md` @ `05467ad` on `main`.
     5	Implementation predecessors: M1a closed at `a59b3f6`; M1b at `e8d80c5`;
     6	M2a at `c1602e5`; M2b at `6068d6f`; M3a at `959becb`; M3b at `6563495`;
     7	M4 closed at the M4 commit landing TranscriptView.tsx + the Open-raw
     8	header anchor.
     9	Designer: UI/UX subagent dispatched 2026-05-10.
    10	
    11	This is a **reference artifact**. The developer subagent re-implements
    12	against the production stack (Bun + Vite + React + TS + per-component
    13	sibling CSS). Nothing in this folder ships to `apps/frontend/`; the
    14	prototype's hex-fallback literals do not contaminate the
    15	`apps/frontend/src/` audit count.
    16	
    17	---
    18	
    19	## 1. Brief
    20	
    21	**M5 is the chunk where the second editorial reading surface lands.**
    22	M2a established the Archive-room language (Fraunces display + Inter
    23	chrome + JetBrains Mono code + oklch-driven warm-paper palette + 83
    24	tokens + the noise overlay). M2b shipped the chrome (tab strip +
    25	header + Metadata + Raw + two placeholders). M3a / M3b shipped the
    26	silent machinery (per-tool parsers, `useParsedSession`, `cacheEpoch`,
    27	`buildSkim`). M4 turned parsed messages into chronological transcript
    28	prose. **M5 turns `parsed.skim` into the editorial outline** — the
    29	second visible UI family in Phase 5.
    30	
    31	The Skim tab is **not the default landing surface** at M5 close
    32	(per Resolved Decision #11 step 3): Transcript stays default until
    33	LLM summaries land in a future phase. Skim becomes functional
    34	this chunk: clicking the tab now lands a real outline of the session
    35	with four block kinds — `user_turn`, `boundary`, `agent_only`,
    36	`oversized_user_message` — instead of the M2b placeholder copy
    37	"Coming in Milestone 5".
    38	
    39	The aesthetic carries forward, untouched. M5 introduces ZERO new
    40	tokens, ZERO new motion vocab, ZERO new fonts, ZERO new hex literals.
    41	Every visible decision in this artifact is a **principled
    42	composition** of the M2a / M2b / M4 tokens, motion, and DOM idioms.
    43	
    44	### 1.1 What M5 ships
    45	
    46	- `SkimView.tsx` + `.css` + `.test.tsx`: the editorial outline
    47	  surface for the Skim tab. State-machine consumer of
    48	  `useParsedSession` (idle / no_raw / loading / error / success /
    49	  truncated). Renders `parsed.skim` (the `SkimBlock[]` from
    50	  `buildSkim`) as a vertical stream of typed blocks.
    51	- `BoundaryRow.tsx` + `.css` + `.test.tsx`: a SHARED component
    52	  extracted from M4's TranscriptView boundary recipe. Both
    53	  TranscriptView (refactored, byte-equivalent DOM) and SkimView
    54	  consume it. This is signature detail #1 — verified at M5 close.
    55	- A skim-block first-paint stagger animation: `opacity 0->1` +
    56	  `translateY(4px -> 0)` per block, 40 ms x min(idx, 8) delay,
    57	  `--motion-disclosure` (200 ms) ease-out duration. Authorized by
    58	  spec §Motion budget row 9 (working/phase-5.md:75 in plan reference).
    59	- Truncation banner + parse-warnings banner reused at byte-equivalent
    60	  visual recipe to M4's banners (per planner Q12).
    61	- TranscriptView gains an additive optional `messageRange={start,
    62	  end}` prop (per planner Q1). When supplied, the body slices
    63	  `parsed.messages.slice(start, end + 1)` before rendering. Default
    64	  behavior unchanged (renders all messages). M4 tests must still
    65	  pass — the prop is BACKWARD-COMPATIBLE.
    66	- `SessionView.tsx` swaps the `<SkimPlaceholder />` for `<SkimView />`.
    67	  `DEFAULT_TAB_ON_SELECTION` stays `"transcript"`.
    68	
    69	### 1.2 What M5 does NOT do
    70	
    71	- It does NOT introduce new tokens. The 83-token canon is reused
    72	  verbatim. If a WCAG pair were to fail with all available tokens,
    73	  implementation halts and escalates to coordinator before adding —
    74	  same protocol M4 documented.
    75	- It does NOT introduce new motion authorizations beyond the spec
    76	  table at `working/phase-5.md:84-95`. The skim-block stagger is
    77	  spec-row 9 (verbatim). Disclosure animations are spec-row 3
    78	  (verbatim). Banner appearance is spec-row 9 (truncation) /
    79	  spec-row 3 (parse-warnings disclosure).
    80	- It does NOT touch Metadata or Raw tabs. M2b + M4's wiring stands.
    81	- It does NOT change `DEFAULT_TAB_ON_SELECTION`. Stays `"transcript"`.
    82	- It does NOT introduce LLM summaries, search, annotations,
    83	  highlights, or virtualization. All deferred to Phase 6+.
    84	- It does NOT modify `useParsedSession`, `buildSkim`, or any
    85	  parser. M5 is a pure consumer of the data layer.
    86	- It does NOT introduce a new motion budget surface. The skim-block
    87	  stagger is spec-table row 9 — already authorized.
    88	
    89	### 1.3 What M5 composes
    90	
    91	| Predecessor surface              | M5 consumption                                                                               |
    92	|----------------------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
    93	| **TranscriptView** (M4)          | Re-mounted under user_turn "Expand to raw messages" disclosures + agent_only block expansion |
    94	| **`useParsedSession`** (M3b)     | Discriminated state-union dispatch; same five non-success state branches                     |
    95	| **`buildSkim`** (M3a)            | `SkimBlock[]` consumed; per-kind switch with TS exhaustiveness on `BlockKind`                |
    96	| **`Tabs`** primitive (M2b)       | SkimView mounts inside the Skim tab panel                                                    |
    97	| **`SessionView`** shell (M2b/M4) | Wiring point; the skim slot in `panelContent` switches from placeholder to SkimView          |
    98	| **27-token consumption set** (M4)| Same tokens reused; ZERO new                                                                 |
    99	| **`renderBodyWithCode`** (M4)    | Re-exported from TranscriptView; SkimView calls it on user_turn body text                    |
   100	
   101	### 1.4 The four block kinds at a glance
   102	
   103	| Block kind                  | Visual signature                                                                                                                              | Disclosure default |
   104	|-----------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|--------------------|
   105	| `user_turn`                 | Accent-tinted reading panel (5 % accent + surface mix). Below it: closed `<details>` "Agent reaction (N messages)". Inside: disabled placeholder + nested `<details>` "Expand to raw messages" mounting a scoped TranscriptView. | Outer + inner `<details>` both CLOSED. |
   106	| `boundary`                  | Full-width 1 px hairline at `--color-border-strong` + centered Fraunces italic small-caps label. 32 px breathing top + bottom. NEVER merged with neighbors. **BYTE-EQUIVALENT to M4's chapter break (signature detail #1).** | n/a (no disclosure). |
   107	| `agent_only`                | Muted bordered panel (hairline at `--color-border`, NO accent tint). Summary: "Agent-only session (N messages)". Expanding mounts scoped TranscriptView spanning `[block.start, block.end]`. | CLOSED. |
   108	| `oversized_user_message`    | Warning-tinted left border in `--color-warn` (4 px). Summary: "Oversized user message (N KB) — collapsed by default". Expanding shows verbatim text in `<pre>` at `--font-mono`. NEVER summarized. | CLOSED. |
   109	
   110	---
   111	
   112	## 2. Aesthetic recap
   113	
   114	M5 inherits the Archive-room language from M2a + M2b + M4. NO new
   115	aesthetic decisions. Six restraints carry the weight, identical to
   116	M4's design.md §1:
   117	
   118	1. **Ink-on-paper rhythm.** Reading content lives at 70ch
   119	   (`var(--measure)`) with `--leading-comfortable` (1.55). Skim
   120	   blocks stack vertically with a three-magnitude rhythm: 24 px
   121	   between same-kind adjacent blocks, 32 px between different-kind
   122	   adjacent blocks, 32 px around boundary blocks. The rhythm token
   123	   set is the same `--space-6` / `--space-8` pair M4 used.
   124	
   125	2. **One tint, used surgically.** User_turn blocks carry a 5 %
   126	   accent tint over the surface — same `color-mix(in srgb,
   127	   var(--color-accent) 5%, var(--color-surface))` recipe M4's
   128	   `.msg-user` panel uses. Agent_only blocks sit on the bare warm
   129	   paper surface with a `--color-border` hairline (no accent tint;
   130	   spec line 693). Oversized blocks carry a 4 px `--color-warn`
   131	   left border (no background tint). Truncation banner carries an
   132	   8 % warn tint with a 3 px `--color-warn` stripe (byte-equivalent
   133	   to M4's banner). Parse-warnings banner sits on
   134	   `--color-surface-raised` with a hairline (also byte-equivalent
   135	   to M4).
   136	
   137	3. **Chapter breaks as page furniture.** `boundary` blocks render
   138	   identically to M4's recipe — 1 px hairlines flanking a Fraunces
   139	   italic small-caps label ("SESSION RESUMED" / "CONVERSATION
   140	   COMPACTED"). 32 px breathing top + bottom. **Byte-equivalent
   141	   between M4 and M5 (signature detail #1).** M5 ships the shared
   142	   `BoundaryRow` component to GUARANTEE byte-equivalence; both
   143	   TranscriptView (refactored, no behavior change) and SkimView
   144	   import the shared component.
   145	
   146	4. **Banners that whisper.** Truncation banner = opacity-only
   147	   entrance + warn stripe + 8 % warn-tinted background, identical
   148	   recipe to M4. Parse-warnings banner = `<details>` element with
   149	   block-size disclosure, identical recipe to M4. Both copy strings
   150	   match M4's verbatim.
   151	
   152	5. **Nested disclosure as the user_turn motif.** Each `user_turn`
   153	   carries TWO `<details>` elements: outer "Agent reaction (N
   154	   messages)" closed by default; inside its body lives the disabled
   155	   placeholder + an inner `<details>` whose summary is "Expand to
   156	   raw messages". Clicking the inner summary mounts the scoped
   157	   TranscriptView. This nested-disclosure shape is editorial: the
   158	   user opts in to read the agent reaction (level 1), then opts in
   159	   AGAIN to see the raw message content (level 2). The placeholder
   160	   sits between levels 1 and 2 carrying the spec-mandated copy.
   161	
   162	6. **First-paint stagger as the only new motion surface.** Skim
   163	   blocks fade in with a 40 ms x min(idx, 8) staggered delay on
   164	   first paint per session. `opacity 0->1` + `transform:
   165	   translateY(4px -> 0)` ONLY. NEVER on `top`, NEVER on
   166	   `padding/width/height`, NEVER on `color/border-color`. Authorized
   167	   by spec §Motion budget row 9 (working/phase-5.md line 75).
   168	   Reduced-motion zeroes the stagger via the global rule.
   169	
   170	The visual continuity statement: a returning user opens a session,
   171	clicks the Skim tab, and sees the editorial outline: each user message
   172	in its own accent-tinted panel; agent reactions folded behind quiet
   173	chrome disclosures; oversized pastes flagged with a warning border;
   174	chapter breaks acting as page furniture. NO new typography. NO new
   175	colors. NO new motion. The outline reads as a printed table of
   176	contents, not a feed.
   177	
   178	References: M2a `apps/frontend/src/styles/tokens.css` lines 1-211 (the
   179	83-token canon); M4 `working/phase-5/designs/m4-transcript/design.md`
   180	lines 14-110 (§1 brief) + lines 478-570 (§4 spacing); M2b
   181	`apps/frontend/src/components/Tabs.tsx` (the disclosure-friendly tab
   182	panel).
   183	
   184	---
   185	
   186	## 3. Token consumption
   187	
   188	M5 consumes exactly the tokens enumerated below. Every token MUST
   189	exist in `apps/frontend/src/styles/tokens.css`. ZERO net additions.
   190	
   191	The list is grouped by usage. Each entry includes (a) the token, (b)
   192	where it's used (selector + block kind), (c) the rationale for
   193	choosing this token over a near-neighbor.
   194	
   195	### 3.1 Color tokens (8 — same set as M4)
   196	
   197	| Token                      | Where                                                                                                                    | Why                                                                                                                                |
   198	|----------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
   199	| `--color-ink`              | user_turn body, oversized header, oversized verbatim `<pre>`, truncation banner copy, parse-warnings summary, retry text | Body-weight ink that hits AAA against `--color-surface` AND `--color-surface-raised` AND the warn-mix banner background.            |
   200	| `--color-ink-muted`        | "Agent reaction" summary, disabled placeholder, agent-only summary, boundary label, system-style chrome rows             | The chrome muted ink. Reads as metadata, not body. Hits AAA against `--color-surface` per M4 T22.                                  |
   201	| `--color-surface`          | agent_only panel, oversized panel, banner backgrounds, every state-branch root                                            | Default warm-paper surface. M2a canon.                                                                                              |
   202	| `--color-surface-raised`   | inline `<code>` and code-fenced `<pre>` inside user_turn body, oversized verbatim `<pre>`, parse-warnings banner shell    | Marks machinery vs prose. Same recipe M4 uses for code-fenced + tool `<pre>`.                                                       |
   203	| `--color-accent`           | user_turn 5 % tint, "Expand to raw messages" affordance, focus-visible outlines                                          | The single accent. M2a-codex-tuned to L=55 light / L=65 dark for AA at 4.5:1.                                                       |
   204	| `--color-border`           | agent_only panel hairline, disabled placeholder 4 px left border, parse-warnings dismiss button, banner shells           | Hairline border for non-chrome shells. Decorative-grade; the SC 1.4.11 obligation lives on the focus outline (not the resting border). |
   205	| `--color-border-strong`    | boundary chapter-break rules (the 1 px hairlines)                                                                         | The boundary's structural rules. M2a-cusp-documented (3.00:1 dark, accepted M4 risk).                                               |
   206	| `--color-warn`             | oversized 4 px left border, truncation banner stripe + 8 % background mix                                                 | Capacity-warning status color. NOT `--color-error` (which is a fatal-state color). Matches M4 truncation banner.                    |
   207	
   208	`--color-error` is NOT consumed by SkimView's success path. It IS
   209	consumed by the `error` state branch (mirrors M4's `state === "error"`
   210	prose) per planner §4.1.

   289	## 4. Block-kind visual treatments
   290	
   291	Each subsection specifies one BlockKind. Every rule is anchored to a
   292	spec line range so a future codex round can verify the recipe against
   293	the source-of-truth.
   294	
   295	### 4.1 `user_turn` — accent-tinted reading panel + nested disclosures
   296	
   297	Spec anchor: `working/phase-5.md:685-689` (verbatim).
   298	
   299	#### 4.1.1 Outer panel
   300	
   301	**Container.** `<li class="skim-block skim-block-user-turn">` with the
   302	inner content tree:
   303	
   304	```html
   305	<li class="skim-block skim-block-user-turn">
   306	  <article class="skim-user-panel">
   307	    <div class="skim-user-body">{user message body, with code-fence rewriting}</div>
   308	  </article>
   309	  <details class="skim-agent-reaction">
   310	    <summary class="skim-agent-reaction-summary">Agent reaction (N messages)</summary>
   311	    <div class="skim-agent-reaction-body">
   312	      <p class="skim-summary-disabled">Summary disabled — generation deferred to a later phase. Use "Expand to raw messages" to read the agent's response inline.</p>
   313	      <details class="skim-expand-raw">
   314	        <summary class="skim-expand-raw-summary">Expand to raw messages</summary>
   315	        <div class="skim-expand-raw-body">
   316	          <TranscriptView row={row} now={now} messageRange={{start: block.start+1, end: block.end}} />
   317	        </div>
   318	      </details>
   319	    </div>
   320	  </details>
   321	</li>
   322	```
   323	
   324	**`<article class="skim-user-panel">`.** Background `color-mix(in
   325	srgb, var(--color-accent) 5%, var(--color-surface))` — byte-equivalent
   326	to M4 `.msg-user` background. Padding `var(--space-6) var(--space-8)`
   327	= 24 px / 32 px (spec line 685: "Panel padding 24 px 32 px"). Border-
   328	radius `var(--radius-md)`. Max-inline-size `var(--measure)` (= 70ch;
   329	spec line 681: "Block content respects the 70ch reading measure").
   330	NO border (the tint differential carries the discrimination).
   331	
   332	**`<div class="skim-user-body">`.** `--font-chrome --text-base
   333	--leading-comfortable --color-ink`. White-space `pre-wrap` so embedded
   334	newlines preserve.
   335	
   336	**Code-fenced segments inside the body.** Reuse M4's
   337	`renderBodyWithCode` helper, re-exported from TranscriptView per
   338	planner Q5. Inline `<code>`: `--font-mono --text-sm` background
   339	`--color-surface-raised` padding `0 var(--space-1)` border-radius
   340	`--radius-sm`. Code block `<pre>`: same background, padding
   341	`var(--space-3) var(--space-4)`, margin `var(--space-3) 0`,
   342	border-radius `--radius-sm`, `overflow-x: auto`, `--font-mono
   343	--text-sm`. The CSS classes (`.msg-code-inline`, `.msg-code-block`)
   344	are inherited from `TranscriptView.css` via the cascade — SkimView
   345	does not redeclare them.
   346	
   347	#### 4.1.2 "Agent reaction (N messages)" disclosure
   348	
   349	Spec literal: `Agent reaction (N messages)` for ALL N (M4
   350	parse-warnings precedent: spec literal beats English grammar).
   351	N = `block.end - block.start` (the messages AFTER the user message
   352	at `block.start`). Examples:
   353	
   354	- N = 0 (user message with no following agent reaction): `Agent reaction (0 messages)`.
   355	- N = 1: `Agent reaction (1 messages)` (grammatically odd; spec literal).
   356	- N = 5: `Agent reaction (5 messages)`.
   357	
   358	**`<details class="skim-agent-reaction">`.** CLOSED by default
   359	(planner Q2). The `<details>` block-size animation inherited via
   360	global `details > *:not(summary)` rule (M2b authorization).
   361	
   362	**`<summary class="skim-agent-reaction-summary">`.**
   363	- Typography: `--font-chrome --text-sm --color-ink-muted`.
   364	- Padding: `var(--space-2) 0`.
   365	- `cursor: pointer`. `list-style: revert` (preserve native disclosure
   366	  triangle).
   367	- NO `text-transform`, NO `letter-spacing` (this is body chrome,
   368	  not an attribution row).
   369	- The summary is the accessible label for the disclosure (a11y §9).
   370	
   371	#### 4.1.3 Disabled placeholder paragraph
   372	
   373	Spec literal (verbatim, spec line 687):
   374	
   375	> Summary disabled — generation deferred to a later phase. Use "Expand to raw messages" to read the agent's response inline.
   376	
   377	**`<p class="skim-summary-disabled">`.**
   378	- Typography: `--font-chrome --text-sm --color-ink-muted`.
   379	- `font-style: italic` (visual cue: this is placeholder, not real content).
   380	- 4 px left border in `--color-border` (spec line 689: "4 px left-border in `--color-border`").
   381	- Padding-inline-start: `var(--space-4)`.
   382	- Margin-block: `var(--space-3) 0`.
   383	- Max-inline-size: `var(--measure)` (the placeholder respects the 70ch
   384	  reading measure too — placed inside the agent-reaction body which
   385	  flows under the user_turn panel).
   386	
   387	The "Expand to raw messages" inside the placeholder text is plain text
   388	with double quotes (matches the spec literal exactly). It is NOT a
   389	hyperlink in the placeholder paragraph; the actual affordance is the
   390	adjacent `<summary>` (next).
   391	
   392	#### 4.1.4 "Expand to raw messages" nested affordance
   393	
   394	Spec line 689: "Followed by an 'Expand to raw messages' affordance
   395	(button styled as a quiet text link): clicking renders a scoped
   396	TranscriptView restricted to the messageIndex range
   397	`[block.start+1, block.end]`."
   398	
   399	Per planner Q3, the affordance is a NESTED `<details>` element
   400	whose `<summary>` reads "Expand to raw messages". The visual
   401	"button styled as a quiet text link" requirement is satisfied via
   402	CSS on the `<summary>`. The semantics of `<details>` give us:
   403	
   404	- Native keyboard handling (Enter / Space toggles).
   405	- Native focus management.
   406	- The block-size disclosure animation (motion-budget authorized).
   407	- No React expanded-state mirror.
   408	
   409	**`<details class="skim-expand-raw">`.** CLOSED by default.
   410	
   411	**`<summary class="skim-expand-raw-summary">`.**
   412	- Typography: `--font-chrome --text-sm`.
   413	- Color: `--color-accent` (the quiet-link recipe; matches M4's
   414	  tool_result "Expand" affordance exactly).
   415	- `cursor: pointer`. `list-style: revert` (preserve disclosure
   416	  triangle so the affordance reads unambiguously as expandable).
   417	- Padding-block: `var(--space-2)`.
   418	- Hover: `text-decoration: underline` (instant — no transition;
   419	  matches M4's `<summary>` hover behavior).
   420	
   421	**`<div class="skim-expand-raw-body">`.** Hosts the scoped
   422	`<TranscriptView />` mount. NO additional padding (TranscriptView
   423	brings its own). Margin-block-start: `var(--space-3)` to separate
   424	from the summary.
   425	
   426	#### 4.1.5 Spec-literal copy invariants
   427	
   428	Each of these strings MUST appear verbatim in `SkimView.tsx`:
   429	
   430	```
   431	Agent reaction (${count} messages)
   432	Summary disabled — generation deferred to a later phase. Use "Expand to raw messages" to read the agent's response inline.
   433	Expand to raw messages
   434	```

   437	
   438	```
   439	rg -nE 'Agent reaction \(' apps/frontend/src/features/sessions/SkimView.tsx
   440	rg -nE 'Summary disabled — generation deferred' apps/frontend/src/features/sessions/SkimView.tsx
   441	rg -nE '"Expand to raw messages"' apps/frontend/src/features/sessions/SkimView.tsx
   442	```

   446	### 4.2 `boundary` — chapter break (signature detail #1, byte-equivalent to M4)
   447	
   448	Spec anchor: `working/phase-5.md:691` + `:66` + Resolved Decision #16
   449	(line 691: "verified at M5 close").
   450	
   451	**This is signature detail #1.** The boundary recipe MUST render the
   452	SAME DOM tree (modulo the optional `style` attribute for stagger) in
   453	both TranscriptView (M4) and SkimView (M5). Per planner Q4, M5
   454	extracts a SHARED `<BoundaryRow>` component to GUARANTEE
   455	byte-equivalence.
   456	
   457	#### 4.2.1 Shared component shape
   458	
   459	```tsx
   460	// apps/frontend/src/features/sessions/BoundaryRow.tsx

exec
/bin/bash -lc "nl -ba working/phase-5/designs/m5-skim/design.md | sed -n '460,720p'" in /home/huwei/ai_codings/distill-portal
 succeeded in 0ms:
   460	// apps/frontend/src/features/sessions/BoundaryRow.tsx
   461	export type BoundarySubtype = "session_resumed" | "compacted";
   462	
   463	export function BoundaryRow({
   464	  subtype,
   465	  staggerIndex,
   466	}: {
   467	  subtype?: BoundarySubtype;
   468	  staggerIndex?: number;
   469	}) {
   470	  const label = subtype === "compacted" ? "CONVERSATION COMPACTED" : "SESSION RESUMED";
   471	  const style = staggerIndex !== undefined ? { animationDelay: `${staggerIndex * 40}ms` } : undefined;
   472	  return (
   473	    <li
   474	      className="boundary-row"
   475	      role="separator"
   476	      aria-orientation="horizontal"
   477	      style={style}
   478	    >
   479	      <span aria-hidden="true" className="boundary-row-rule boundary-row-rule-start" />
   480	      <span className="boundary-row-label">{label}</span>
   481	      <span aria-hidden="true" className="boundary-row-rule boundary-row-rule-end" />
   482	    </li>
   483	  );
   484	}
   485	```
   486	
   487	Rendered DOM tree (both contexts):
   488	
   489	```html
   490	<li class="boundary-row" role="separator" aria-orientation="horizontal">
   491	  <span aria-hidden="true" class="boundary-row-rule boundary-row-rule-start"></span>
   492	  <span class="boundary-row-label">SESSION RESUMED</span>
   493	  <span aria-hidden="true" class="boundary-row-rule boundary-row-rule-end"></span>
   494	</li>
   495	```
   496	
   497	The only context-specific difference: SkimView passes `staggerIndex`
   498	so the wrapping `<li>` carries `style="animation-delay: ${idx*40}ms"`;
   499	TranscriptView omits the prop. The flat `<li role="separator">` shape
   500	matches M4's design.md §2.1 + §3.6 + §12.2 verbatim.
   501	
   502	#### 4.2.2 BoundaryRow.css recipe (extracted from M4)
   503	
   504	```css
   505	/* apps/frontend/src/features/sessions/BoundaryRow.css */
   506	
   507	.boundary-row {
   508	  display: grid;
   509	  grid-template-columns: 1fr auto 1fr;
   510	  align-items: center;
   511	  gap: var(--space-4);
   512	  margin-block: var(--space-8);  /* 32 px breathing top + bottom */
   513	  list-style: none;
   514	  inline-size: 100%;
   515	  /* NO max-inline-size: rules reach edge-to-edge of the stream container,
   516	     while the stream itself is constrained to var(--measure). */
   517	}
   518	
   519	.boundary-row-rule {
   520	  block-size: 1px;
   521	  inline-size: 100%;
   522	  background: var(--color-border-strong);
   523	}
   524	
   525	.boundary-row-label {
   526	  font-family: var(--font-display);
   527	  font-size: var(--text-sm);
   528	  color: var(--color-ink-muted);
   529	  font-style: italic;
   530	  font-variant: small-caps;
   531	  letter-spacing: 0.18em;
   532	  padding: 0 var(--space-3);
   533	}
   534	```
   535	
   536	**Byte-equivalent to M4's `.msg-boundary*` declarations** (cite
   537	`working/phase-5/designs/m4-transcript/design.md:384-406`). The
   538	class-name change `.msg-boundary` -> `.boundary-row` makes the
   539	selector neutral between Transcript-message and Skim-block contexts.
   540	
   541	#### 4.2.3 BoundaryRow extraction surface
   542	
   543	The M4 refactor:
   544	
   545	1. Lift the `BoundaryMessage` body from `TranscriptView.tsx` lines
   546	   362-384 (the inline `<li role="separator">` recipe) into the new
   547	   `BoundaryRow.tsx`.
   548	2. Lift the `.msg-boundary*` declarations from `TranscriptView.css`
   549	   into `BoundaryRow.css` as `.boundary-row*`.
   550	3. Update `TranscriptView.tsx`'s `BoundaryMessage` to render
   551	   `<BoundaryRow subtype={msg.boundarySubtype} />` (no
   552	   `staggerIndex` — Transcript doesn't stagger).
   553	4. Update `TranscriptView.css` rhythm rules at lines 168-172:
   554	   replace `.msg-boundary` with `.boundary-row`.
   555	5. SkimView imports `BoundaryRow` directly.
   556	
   557	**M4 invariance:** the rendered DOM tree is byte-equivalent.
   558	TranscriptView's existing boundary tests (existence test ID #11 per
   559	M4 plan §8) still pass; the assertions either target
   560	`getByRole("separator")` (class-agnostic) or update from
   561	`.msg-boundary*` to `.boundary-row*` (one-line selector edit).
   562	
   563	#### 4.2.4 Spec-literal copy
   564	
   565	Boundary subtype mapping:
   566	- `subtype === "session_resumed"` -> `SESSION RESUMED`
   567	- `subtype === "compacted"` -> `CONVERSATION COMPACTED`
   568	- `subtype === undefined` -> `SESSION RESUMED` (default, defensive).
   569	
   570	Strings are uppercase in source (NOT relying on `text-transform`).
   571	Fraunces' small-caps glyph set is exercised on uppercase input — the
   572	OpenType font feature handles the rest.
   573	
   574	Verification (run at implementation close):
   575	
   576	```
   577	rg -nE 'SESSION RESUMED|CONVERSATION COMPACTED' apps/frontend/src/features/sessions/BoundaryRow.tsx
   578	```
   579	
   580	Both literals must appear (one in the ternary).
   581	
   582	### 4.3 `agent_only` — muted bordered panel + scoped TranscriptView reveal
   583	
   584	Spec anchor: `working/phase-5.md:693`.
   585	
   586	#### 4.3.1 Panel shape
   587	
   588	```html
   589	<li class="skim-block skim-block-agent-only">
   590	  <details class="skim-agent-only">
   591	    <summary class="skim-agent-only-summary">Agent-only session (N messages)</summary>
   592	    <div class="skim-agent-only-body">
   593	      <TranscriptView row={row} now={now} messageRange={{start: block.start, end: block.end}} />
   594	    </div>
   595	  </details>
   596	</li>
   597	```
   598	
   599	#### 4.3.2 Visual recipe
   600	
   601	**`<details class="skim-agent-only">`.** CLOSED by default (spec line
   602	693: "collapsed by default"; PRD line 256). Border `1px solid
   603	var(--color-border)`. Border-radius `var(--radius-md)`. Padding
   604	`var(--space-3) var(--space-4)` (12 px / 16 px — tight chrome
   605	disclosure padding, NOT the user_turn 24/32 reading-panel padding).
   606	Background `var(--color-surface)` (default surface; spec line 693:
   607	"NO accent tint"). Max-inline-size `var(--measure)` (the disclosure
   608	respects the reading column).
   609	
   610	**`<summary class="skim-agent-only-summary">`.**
   611	- `--font-chrome --text-sm --color-ink-muted` (spec line 693: "summary
   612	  line in `--font-chrome` `--text-sm` `--color-ink-muted`").
   613	- `cursor: pointer`. `list-style: revert`.
   614	- NO uppercasing or letter-spacing.
   615	
   616	**`<div class="skim-agent-only-body">`.** Hosts the scoped
   617	`<TranscriptView />`. Margin-block-start `var(--space-3)`. NO inner
   618	padding (TranscriptView brings its own stream-container padding).
   619	
   620	#### 4.3.3 Spec-literal pluralization
   621	
   622	Count formula: `block.end - block.start + 1` (inclusive both ends).
   623	Spec literal for ALL N (matches "Agent reaction" precedent):
   624	
   625	- N = 0 (empty-stream sentinel: `block.start = 0, block.end = -1`):
   626	  `Agent-only session (0 messages)`.
   627	- N = 1: `Agent-only session (1 messages)` (spec literal beats grammar).
   628	- N = 12: `Agent-only session (12 messages)`.
   629	
   630	#### 4.3.4 Empty-stream sentinel handling
   631	
   632	Per `buildSkim.ts` line 55: when `parsed.messages` is empty,
   633	`buildSkim` emits `{ kind: "agent_only", start: 0, end: -1, meta: {
   634	empty: 1 } }`. The count formula gives N = 0, summary text "Agent-only
   635	session (0 messages)". Expanding the disclosure mounts a scoped
   636	TranscriptView with `messageRange={{start: 0, end: -1}}`. Per planner
   637	Q10, TranscriptView's body slice clamps `hi < lo` to an empty slice
   638	and renders the spec-anchored "No messages parsed." copy. This
   639	satisfies spec line 697 ("never silently blank"): both the summary
   640	line ("Agent-only session (0 messages)") and the expanded body
   641	("No messages parsed.") carry copy.
   642	
   643	Verification (run at implementation close):
   644	
   645	```
   646	rg -nE 'Agent-only session \(' apps/frontend/src/features/sessions/SkimView.tsx
   647	```
   648	
   649	Must match exactly once (the JSX template).

   652	
   653	Spec anchor: `working/phase-5.md:695`.

   660	    <summary class="skim-oversized-summary">Oversized user message ({sizeKB} KB) — collapsed by default</summary>
   661	    <div class="skim-oversized-body">

   663	    </div>
   664	  </details>
   665	</li>
   666	```
   667	
   668	#### 4.4.2 Visual recipe
   669	
   670	**`<li class="skim-block skim-block-oversized">`.**
   671	- Border-inline-start: `4px solid var(--color-warn)` (spec line 695:
   672	  "warning-tinted left border ... so the user notices the size signal").
   673	- Padding-inline-start: `var(--space-4)` (so the body content does
   674	  not flush against the warn stripe).
   675	- NO background tint (the stripe is the only color signal).
   676	- Max-inline-size `var(--measure)`.
   677	
   678	**`<details class="skim-oversized">`.** CLOSED by default (spec line
   679	695: "collapsed by default"; PRD line 257).
   680	
   681	**`<summary class="skim-oversized-summary">`.**
   682	- `--font-chrome --text-sm --color-ink` (NOT `--color-ink-muted` —
   683	  the oversized header is a warning signal, not muted chrome).
   684	- `cursor: pointer`. `list-style: revert`.
   685	- The em-dash inside the summary copy is U+2014 EM DASH (single
   686	  Unicode character). The literal:
   687	  `Oversized user message (${sizeKB} KB) — collapsed by default`.

   692	  recipe).

   694	- Border-radius `--radius-sm`.
   695	- `white-space: pre`. `overflow-x: auto`.
   696	- NEVER summarized (spec line 695: "NEVER summarized"; PRD line 257).
   697	  The full `parsed.messages[block.start].text` is rendered verbatim.
   698	
   699	#### 4.4.3 KB conversion
   700	
   701	Per planner Q11: `Math.round(meta.sizeBytes / 1024)`.
   702	
   703	- `meta.sizeBytes = 65537` -> `Math.round(64.0009)` = `64` -> "64 KB".
   704	- `meta.sizeBytes = 70000` -> `Math.round(68.359)` = `68` -> "68 KB".
   705	- `meta.sizeBytes = 102400` -> `Math.round(100.0)` = `100` -> "100 KB".
   706	- `meta.sizeBytes = undefined` -> defensive `0` -> "0 KB".
   707	
   708	#### 4.4.4 Spec-literal copy
   709	
   710	Verbatim:
   711	
   712	```
   713	Oversized user message (${sizeKB} KB) — collapsed by default
   714	```
